## 3. Finding

`POST /api/tokenize` is absent on Ollama 0.40.12. The docs put the input count in `prompt_eval_count` on the done frame of `POST /api/generate` or `POST /api/chat`. The body needs `model` and the text (`prompt`, or `messages` for chat). `stream: false` returns one object. That request loads the model when the process list is empty and it generates output tokens. `options.num_predict` 1 capped the probe at one output token. `options.num_predict` 0 did not. `keep_alive` 0 unloads when the call ends. The count includes the rendered template. A later plate that wants this number on the glass has to name that live completion. This plate does not.

## 3. Edits

Token Calc stores its preferred size as its maximum once, at install, before the live font. That cap is removed. `applyMenuFont` calls the private method `fitMenuWidth` after the font is seated.

`fitMenuWidth` clears the minimum, preferred, and maximum on each bar item, measures the live preferred size, and stores that size as the minimum and the preferred size. The maximum width is that width. The maximum height stays open so the item does not stretch across the bar. Each menu popup clears its preferred size and takes the new preferred size as its size. `setPopupSize` is not called.

When the menu width plus the preferred width of the controls that share its row, plus the frame insets, is wider than the frame, the frame grows to that width. The minimum width rises only when that sum exceeds the current minimum. The 640 by 480 floor stays.

## 3. Edits

The root cause is the arm. `openMood` called `findCell(focusedCellId)` and returned before `setVisible` when that id was null, when the cell was gone, or when the cell was not readable. `focusedCellId` is set on a card press and is never cleared. The only signal was the status word no cell. `targetCell` already falls through to the last cell on the showing canvas. Mood ignored that fall-through.

The private method `moodCard` keeps an armed readable cell. When the arm misses, it uses `targetCell` when that cell is readable, otherwise the last readable cell on the showing canvas. When no readable cell remains, the status still says no cell and the dialog stays closed. The cloud gate and the connector gate stay where they are.

Output only keeps the assistant half. Section 3A is that rule. The Java keeps `tokenActive` until a later sentence names the build.

## 3. The one transcript

A transcript is ordered turns, oldest first, from the start through the armed construct, inclusive. A turn is one user half and one assistant half. Either half may be empty.

One walk builds it. A merge arm, a member arm, and an ordinary arm use that walk.

The walk reads `orderedCells` of the current canvas. The order is `cellOrder`, then any cell that list omitted. The walk does not call `snapGroup`. It does not write `cellOrder`. It does not call `armFocus`. It does not persist. It does not set `executionStatus`. It does not clear `chainQueue`.

The selected card is `targetCell`. An empty canvas has no card, and the estimate paints 0. A canvas with cards and no `focusedCellId` uses the fall-through `targetCell` already uses, the last cell in `canvas.cells`. The stop is that card's index in `orderedCells` when the card has no group id. The stop is the last index of that group id when the card has one. Cells after the stop stay out.

A cell with no group id emits one ordinary turn. A group id emits one turn when its first cell at or before the stop is reached. That turn gathers every cell of that group id at an index at or before the stop. A later cell of a group already emitted is skipped. Members are not also ordinary turns. An Excluded merge stays one group turn. An unreadable merge stays one group turn. The members do not become ordinary cards.

Member prompts are the cells with `columnPosition` above 0, in that canvas order. Empty prompt text stays out of the join. A newline sits between the prompts that remain, the same join `memberPrompts` uses. The walk uses that gate. It does not call `activeMemberPrompts`. `stripJoinBanners` runs on the merge reply. Member prompts stay as stored. A member line that begins with `JOIN ` stays that member's text.

An ordinary card is one turn. The raw user half is the full user text. On the armed card the live draft is that half. The raw assistant half is the full assistant text when the card already holds a reply. Section 3A decides which halves the count keeps. The reply stays in the raw transcript because the transcript is the conversation through that construct. A Run still sends the working set from `assemble`. The number and the send are allowed to differ.

## 3. The one transcript

Section 3B is the arming requirement. A click on the merge and a click on a member of that group arm the same unit: the turns above the group, then the group as that one turn. A click on a card below the group includes the group as that full turn. A click on a card above the group stops at that card. The group stays outside that transcript until the arm reaches it.

The counted string is the rendered transcript. Full turn text. No role prefix. No ellipsis. Turns concatenate in order. A non-empty half is followed by one newline. An empty half adds nothing. No extra blank line is inserted between turns. The character length is that rendered string. An empty rendering has length 0. Mood, when on, is the head.

A pipe is resolved by `resolvePipes` against `sourcesBefore(orderedCells, stop card, cloud)`. The stop card is the selected card when it has no group id. The stop card is the merge sink when the selected card has a group id. That list is the pipe index. `k` stays 1-based into that list. The list is not the transcript. A return that starts with `pipe:` contributes no text for that half. The other half of the turn still follows its mark. The estimate does not call `assemble`. It does not mark the cell failed. ## 3A. Modifiers

Structure runs first. State runs second. Both run for every construct through the armed construct. The estimator sees the halves that remain.

A structural modifier says how a construct becomes turns.

An ordinary card is one turn. The raw user half is the full user text. On the armed card the live draft is that half. The raw assistant half is the full assistant text when the card holds a reply.

A merge group is one turn. Each member's state modifier gates that member's prompt. Prompts that remain join by a newline. That join is the group's user half. The merge card's state modifier gates the reply after `stripJoinBanners`. That reply is the group's assistant half. The merge mark gates the reply. A member mark gates that member's prompt. One mark does not stand in for the other.

## 3A. Modifiers

A state modifier says which halves count. The seated four match the gates `appendCell` already uses on a send.

Included. The user half stays. The assistant half stays.

Excluded. The user half stays out. The assistant half stays out. The walk still visits the card. The rendered transcript gains no text from it. The block count does not gain a turn.

Input only. The user half stays. The assistant half stays out. A live draft on an Input only card counts.

Output only. The assistant half stays. The user half stays out. The prompt on an Output only card stays out, including a live draft.

The same gates sit on a group. A member prompt counts when that member is Included or Input only, which is the gate in `memberPrompts`. A merge reply counts when the merge is Included or Output only, which is the gate in `appendMergedTurn`. An Excluded merge leaves the cards above it in the transcript. The group stays one turn. The reply stays out because the mark is Excluded. Member prompts still follow each member's own mark. The members are not emitted as ordinary cards.

A construct whose marks keep no half adds no turn. A construct whose marks keep a half adds one turn, including a half whose text is empty. A blank ephemeral card adds no turn, including when that card is the selected card.

When `cloudTarget(outboundTarget(selected card))` is true, every ephemeral cell other than the selected card contributes `ExcludedModifier`, then its mark. A local target does not add that modifier. The selected card is not dropped for being ephemeral. `outboundTarget` is the card's execution target, and an empty target reads as `ollama`.

`assemble` stays the Run. The estimate is this transcript. The send gates halves and stops before the armed reply. The estimate includes the armed reply when the armed mark keeps that half. The two strings are allowed to differ.

## 3. The cause

The four marks are JToggleButtons in one ButtonGroup. stateButton writes cell.state inside the action, then calls armHold, persistQuiet, rebuildTabs, and refreshInspector. On Zulu 26, ToggleButtonModel.setPressed(false) fires that action only while the model is still armed. BasicButtonListener.focusLost calls setPressed(false) and then setArmed(false). mouseExited calls setArmed(false) and writes nothing.

installImportPopup runs on the button. Its mousePressed calls armFocus, then focusCanvas on an ordinary card or focusGroupCell on a grouped card. That runs while the button is armed and pressed, so focusLost runs the action inside the press. rebuildTabs calls canvasTabs.removeAll and builds every card again. applyUiFont refits the notebook. restoreHold runs later, moves the viewport, and focusHeader lands on the collapse button. The matching release is not delivered. The button is already gone.

## 3. The cause

Ctrl-Shift I, E, P, and O write the state and then call the same rebuild. They are the same seat. A JButton is the same miss. DefaultButtonModel fires the action from setPressed(false) while armed, and focusLost uses the same order. mouseExited still clears armed first. The seat is the press, off the button model.

## 4. The seat

seatMark(cell, state) is the one method. It writes cell.state. It paints the four faces from that state. It repaints the card, so the ghost follows EXCLUDED and clears when the state is any other mark. It calls persistQuiet. It may call refreshInspector. It does not call rebuildTabs. It does not call armHold. It does not call focusHeader. It does not move the viewport.

The four marks are plain buttons. They are not a ButtonGroup. They are not JToggleButtons. setFocusable(false). setRequestFocusEnabled(false). No ActionListener. The button's own mousePressed calls seatMark for that letter, then consumes the gesture. The release does not call seatMark. A second press of the letter already seated writes that same state again and leaves the deck in place.

installImportPopup still walks the button. When the source is a mark, that listener may call armFocus and may still open the import popup. It does not call focusCanvas. It does not call focusGroupCell. It does not request focus.

The mark face reads cell.state. The seated letter paints with PLATE_SELECT and PLATE_SELECT_INK. The other three paint the idle plate. Hover and press stay the plate hover and the plate press. ButtonModel.isSelected is not the paint. A focus change cannot clear the letter.

Ctrl-Shift I, E, P, and O call seatMark and return. That listener does not call rebuildTabs.

The gesture updates the live card. A rebuild that some other gesture still needs stays on that gesture. A mark does not post one. If a later reading still wants a rebuild after a mark, that rebuild is invokeLater after the current mouse event returns, and it is not the path this seat takes.

## 3. The cause

A canvas send that did not fit the AMWP frame built the body again without the mood and still sent. A short canvas and a cleared canvas fit, so they carried the mood. A long canvas, a switch turned back on over a long canvas, and a Transfer Conversation that had copied many SSH turns onto the canvas, did not fit, and went out without the mood.

sshLine read the checkbox and the saved line. It did not read the footer switch. The footer off left the announced line the same, so the shell sent nothing and the SSH mood stayed. The footer on, with the box already checked and the pane already told, did the same. Clearing the SSH session seats the server line onto an empty region, which is why a cleared SSH session activated.

Transfer from SSH onto the canvas copies user and assistant cells. The mood is not a cell. The next canvas execute is the seat, and the too-large retry was dropping it.

Transfer from the canvas onto Roux replaces the turns and then seats the server's previous mood line again. The client was not sending the gate after that ok.

The activate sentence was one line after the mood. The engagement he asked for is the opener, the constructed mood, and the closer.

## 4. The seat

canvasSystems, while the footer switch is on and the saved line is non-blank, returns three texts. The first is OPENER. The second is normalize of the saved line. The third is CLOSER. Off, or a blank line, returns an empty list. moodHead joins those three with newlines.

OPENER is: I'm opening a window with this atmosphere… would you like to step into it with me?

CLOSER is: Shape your replies around this mood — let it breathe through the words.

The constructed mood is the saved line the desk already normalizes. The example paragraph stays an example. It is not a constant.

LEGACY keeps the old sentence: Activate the mood above. Use that mood as the shape of your responses. A later seat still drops that row, so an existing transcript does not keep the stale line and does not gain a second triple. adopt and porchRules still read a legacy pair.

sshLine takes the checkbox, the footer switch, and the saved line. Both gates on, and a line saved, returns normalize of that line. Either gate off returns null. sshMoodLineFor passes the checkbox, moodOn, and the saved line. The status strip follows that line.

The canvas execute keeps the triple in the working set. A frame that still does not fit fails too-large and does not run. The send does not drop the triple to fit. History stays. A clear that already fit still fits.

seat writes the host, then OPENER, then the constructed mood, then CLOSER, then the turns. A null line clears the triple and keeps the turns. The strip drops OPENER, CLOSER, and LEGACY. It treats the preceding row as the mood when the next row is CLOSER or LEGACY. A host followed by OPENER stays. The mood followed by CLOSER or LEGACY leaves, and the text is remembered so a later copy of that mood leaves too.

A connect still announces through announceBody. The switch off makes the want line empty, so a told pane receives shell_mood_clear. The switch on, with the box checked and a line saved, changes the want line, so the pane receives shell_mood and setMoodLine writes the triple into the transcript already stored. A pane already told that same line sends nothing. A new connection starts untold. A restart starts untold.

toggleMood still flips the switch, saves, and calls noteMoodRelay. The relay walks the panes.

applyTransferReplace, when the result starts with ok, calls noteMoodRelay before the status line. The server may still hold the previous line until that frame arrives. Transfer from Roux onto the canvas still stores user and assistant cells. The triple is not a cell. The next canvas execute carries it from the switch.

The glass writes OPENER, the field text, and CLOSER when the field holds a line. The footer switch does not gate the glass. The field text is the constructed line, the draft while the dialog is open and the saved line otherwise.

The inspector shape line shows CLOSER. The checkbox tip says the box makes mood available for that user's SSH panes, that SSH mood is on when the box is checked and the switch is on, that the switch off turns SSH mood off while the box can stay checked, and that an unchecked box leaves SSH without the mood while the switch is on. The switch tip says a send carries the opener, the saved mood, and the closer, that SSH mood turns off with the switch, and that SSH mood comes on with the switch when that user's box is checked.

The same text only moves the cursor. undo and redo swap that stored text. The caller writes text and cursor into the widget that is live. applying stops the listener from pushing the write it just applied. sync remembers a rebuilt widget without pushing. discard drops the stack.

A canvas cell id maps to one EditMemory. The map is RAM. A deleted cell drops its seat. Clear turns drops the seats of the cells that left. The prompt binds Ctrl-Z and Shift-Ctrl-Z while it is focused. The initial text is synced, not recorded. Import through setText is one step.

| Pkg | Name | Lands |

## 5. Package 1. Effort table

- `grok-4.7` and `grok-4.6`: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`. Start `high`.
- `grok-4.5`: `low`, `medium`, `high`. Start `high`.
- `grok-4.20-multi-agent-0309`: those four levels. Start `high`.
- Every other id, including an empty id: `high` only. Start `high`. Do not invent `low`, `medium`, or `xhigh` for `grok-4.3`, `grok-4.20-0309-reasoning`, `grok-4.20-0309-non-reasoning`, or `grok-build-0.1`.
- The string `omit` leaves the rows, the factory, the copy, the encode skip, and the service skip. It is not a level.
- `normalizeEffort(model, value)` returns the value when it is still a row, otherwise the start. Empty and `omit` return the start.

- `fillModels`: a non-empty chosen id still selects that id. An empty chosen id does not select `grok-4.3`. The combo stays unselected.
- `write`: an empty model never becomes the standing default. A non-empty model becomes standing when `alsoDefault` is true or `standing()` is null. `copyOnto` keeps calling `write(cell, source, false)`. A copied non-empty model with no standing default therefore becomes standing. An empty model does not.
- A new entry for a cell with no document is constructed with an empty model and effort `high`. `factory()` stays the shape of a row that already has a model. A stored `grok-4.3` stays.

## 6. Package 2. Holder and the think key

- Two families on one cell keep both values. A family switch does not clear the holder.

- `think_stream` stays the Grok stream flag. `assemble` does not put Ollama think on it. Update the field comment that still says the flag is also Ollama think.
- The router parameter `boolean ollamaThink` becomes `String think`. No new parameter beside it. `complete(target, prompt)` passes `""`. Call sites that pass `false` pass `""`. Call sites that pass `true` pass `"true"`.

- The holder keeps `ollamaLevel` when the box is turned off. The next on sends that level again.
- `grokOn` is session RAM. No yaml key. A shell restart clears it. Effort, stream, temperature, max tokens, burner, and the model stay on the document.
- A copied Grok document copies the source cell's `grokOn` onto the destination holder.
- `paintThinkDelta`: when the cell provider is grok and `grokOn` is off, drop kind `t`. Kind `c` and the finished `CHANNEL_STREAM` `TYPE_DATA` reply still paint. An ollama cell's kind `t` still paints. The server still sends the Grok delta when stream is true. The strip stays the one `JTextArea`.
- The server reads `think` only when the provider is ollama. After a successful Ollama complete, send kind `t` when the think string is non-empty and `thinkingText` is non-empty. The old `req.thinkStream && ollama` test leaves.

## 7. Package 3. Grok body and the probe level

- `assemble` copies `normalizeEffort` onto `reasoningEffort` for a grok cell. An Ollama frame does not carry `reasoning_effort`.
- `encodeExecute` writes `reasoning_effort` when the value is non-empty. The `omit` skip leaves.
- `requestBody` always puts `reasoning_effort`. An empty value is `high` before the JSON is built. The service does not consult the model table.
- `probeBody(model, effort)` adds `reasoning_effort`. Empty effort is `high`. Temperature stays off. `max_completion_tokens` stays 16. `stream` stays false. The message stays `Hello`.

## 7. Package 3. Grok body and the probe level

- `startProbe(model, effort)` and `LlmRouter.startGrokProbe(model, effort)` take the level. `ProbeGate.admit(model, effort)` passes that level only into `startGrokProbe` when this call is the leader. The valid flag stays the API key. A later grok cell does not probe again. Its own `requestBody` sends its own level.

- Provider grok with an empty model stays `no-model` before air-gap. That order stays.

## 8. Package 4. Ollama id and ThinkLevel

- Widen `complete(Prompt, boolean)` to `complete(Prompt, String model, String think)`. The boolean method does not remain beside it.

- Build the options object when a model id is present, or `numCtx > 0`, or think is on. A configured `num-ctx` still rides.
- Think string empty: leave the option unset. `ThinkBoolean.DISABLED` is not sent.
- Think string `true`: `setThinkOption(ThinkOption.ThinkBoolean.ENABLED)`.
- Think string `low`, `medium`, or `high`: `new ThinkOption.ThinkLevel(level)`.
- Any other string: leave the option unset. Do not throw on the chat path. The show filter in package 5 is what keeps a foreign string off the control.
- spring-ai stays 1.1.8. Do not follow a later branch that removes these setters. There is no second Ollama chat client. The call stays inside the Roux hold.

Empty uses `http://localhost:11434`. Do not edit `application.yaml`. Do not call `OllamaLoadCommand`. The path is `/api/show`, not `/api/generate`.

## 9. Package 5. Show frame

- In `handle`, after the body is stripped and before the `inFlight` check: when the type is `TYPE_DATA`, the channel is `CHANNEL_A`, and the first line starts with `show:`, run the show and return. Do not enter `execute`. Do not acquire `executeSlots`. Do not mark the cell busy, failed, or running. A run already in flight stays in flight. The show does not answer `cell-busy`.
- The model id is the trimmed text after `show:`. POST `{"model": id}` with Jackson. Connect 10 seconds. Read 10 seconds.
- Reply on `CHANNEL_A` `TYPE_DATA` for that cell id. Body starts with `show_result:` and the id. Then one `value:` line per kept string. A miss is `show_result:` and no value lines. It is not `TYPE_ERROR` and it is not `CHANNEL_STREAM`.
- Read top-level `thinking.values`. Drop booleans. Keep a string only when it is `low`, `medium`, or `high`, in the order returned. Do not apply `thinking.default`. A missing object, an empty list, a boolean-only list, or an HTTP miss yields no values.
- A user prompt stays inside `working_set`. A body that starts with `cell_id:` is an execute, including the mood shaper. Those bodies do not enter the show branch.
- `acceptFrame` handles `CHANNEL_A` `TYPE_DATA` whose body starts with `show_result:`. Apply it on the EDT only while the modal is open on that cell and the id still matches. A stale reply is dropped. Other `CHANNEL_A` `TYPE_DATA` stays unpainted. The cell answer path stays `CHANNEL_STREAM`.
- The client sends the frame off the EDT, on id-field focus loss and when the Ollama Thinking checkbox is turned on, and only when the id is non-empty. It does not send on each character. No AMWP session means the client does not send and the effort rows stay hidden. There is no run banner.
- The stored level stays selected when it is still a returned row. Otherwise the effort pull-down has no selection until the user picks one. The checkbox still works. Rows hide when the reply has no values.

## 10. Package 6. Modal, label, and stored strings

`openGrokDialog` becomes the modal. Title `Model Config`. No second `JDialog`. OK and Cancel leave. Window close leaves the cell as last written. The `windowClosed` restore of the prior target leaves. `grokDialogOpen` stays the one flag. Application modality already blocks delete while the modal is open.

- One `Model` button on the main bar of every readable card. Title `Model`. The unreadable card returns before the button and the label. It keeps `load error` and the disabled `?`.
- The family pull-down is the first control. Rows start with Ollama and Grok. One pane shows. The Grok pane is the form the dialog already builds, plus a Thinking checkbox. The Ollama pane is the model id, a Thinking checkbox, and the effort pull-down. `fillModels` stays the model table.
- A new cell, a cleared canvas, and an empty target open on Ollama.
- A combo, a checkbox, and the family pull-down call `persistQuiet` at once. Temperature writes on focus loss when the text is a finite double. Max tokens writes on focus loss when the text is an int of 1 or more. A bad number keeps the last good value and the status line says `settings`.
- A target change updates `executionTarget`, the group-bar field, and the label. A Thinking change updates the holder and leaves the target string as it is.
- The free-text field still updates the cell in memory and the label on each key. It does not persist on each character. `syncGrokModelFromField` still runs on focus loss.
- Ollama with an empty id stores an empty target. It does not store the word `ollama` or `ollama/`. Ollama with an id stores `ollama/` plus the trimmed id. The id updates memory and the label on each change and persists on focus loss. A typed `ollama` or `ollama/` normalizes to empty on focus loss. Do not case-fold Ollama tags.
- Grok with a document model stores `grok/` plus that id. Grok with no model stores `grok/`. The label reads `grok`. The pull-down does not store a bare `grok`. Hand-typed `grok` still parses as provider grok with an empty model.
- A provider that is neither ollama nor grok leaves the pull-down unselected and both panes hidden. Opening the modal does not rewrite that target.
- Typing a grok target adopts the document: nearest earlier cell, else standing, else a new empty-model entry with effort `high`. It does not open a second dialog. `adoptGrok` returns when the cell already has a document. `nearestGrok` still ignores an empty model.
- Selecting Grok with no document fills from the nearest earlier cell, else standing, and writes at once. A copied effort that is not a defined level is written as `high`. Close that synchronized block after clear, add, and the still-mine check that follows add. `runPool`, probe admit, `llmRouter.complete`, and the reply send run after the lock is released. clear and add stay one pair, so the region is the last writer's whole list. ## 3. The edit

`AetherisNotebookPanel.showViewModal`.

Ctrl-F opens the bar and focuses the field. When the bar is already open and the field has text, Ctrl-F moves to the next match and wraps to the start. When the field is empty, Ctrl-F focuses the field. Esc hides the bar, removes the highlights, and returns focus to the reply. Esc leaves the window open. `viewModalOpen` stays true until the window closes.

Each keystroke on the Swing thread marks every match in the reply text already in the area. The compare is `regionMatches` with ignore-case. The reply is not copied into a lower-cased string. An empty field clears the highlights and does not select the reply. No match sets the count to none. The error slide stays out of this search.

Every stored match is painted with a highlighter. The other matches use `0x6b5a1e`. The current match uses `0xf9a825` and is also the selection, scrolled into view. ^ moves to the previous match and wraps to the end. v moves to the next match and wraps to the start. The field tip names type-to-highlight, Ctrl-F for the next match, and Esc to close the bar. The ^ tip names the previous match and the wrap to the end. The v tip names the next match and the wrap to the start.

The search reads that reply, including JOIN lines in a merge. The canvas search stays the canvas search. The field is not filled from a selection. At most 2000 matches are stored and painted. Past that the count says there are more, and the buttons walk the stored set.

No lambdas. Anonymous classes for the key actions, the buttons, and the document listener.

## 1. Goal

Stop paying Heavy for two copies of the same column. The first prompt of a pool session carries the working set. A later prompt on that session carries the tail. ## 2. Out of this build

- A change to the walker, the five pool reasons, the child gate, the semaphore of 3, the idle reap, `num-ctx`, air-gap, or the Grok URL.

## 3. The edit

## 3. The edit

`AmwpToon`.

`ExecuteBody` gains `poolBoot`. Encode writes `pool_boot:` when the field is non-empty. Decode reads it. Absent means empty. An empty boot on the server keeps a new session on every prompt, which is the earlier behavior.

`PoolChat.prompt`.

The method gains the boot string between the text and the think flag. One implementor.

`AmwpFacade.runPool`.

The call passes `req.poolBoot` and the same `poolPromptText` string it already builds. The slice happens inside the binding. The façade still hands the whole string across.

`PoolChatServer`.

The child gains `heldBoot`, `flatHeld`, and `holdValid`. They live on that SSH user's child, beside `sessionId`. The prompt gate still wraps the whole prompt, so two pool cells for one user stay in order and the hold updates on that same stack.

A hold extends when all of these are true: `holdValid` is set, the boot string is non-empty and equals `heldBoot`, the process is alive and initialized, `sessionId` is non-empty, `flatHeld` is non-empty, and the new working-set string starts with `flatHeld` and is longer than `flatHeld`. The child then skips `session/new` and sends `session/prompt` with the substring after `flatHeld`.

Every other prompt drops the hold, calls `session/new`, and sends the whole string. Dropping the hold clears `holdValid`, `flatHeld`, `heldBoot`, and `sessionId`. The process stays up. `initialize` still runs once on that process.

On a clean success the binding stores the boot, sets `holdValid` when the boot is non-empty, and sets `flatHeld` to the whole working-set string plus one assistant block when the stored reply is non-empty. The block is the same three-part shape `poolPromptText` uses for an assistant turn: the word `assistant`, a newline, the reply text, a newline, and a blank line. An empty reply adds no block. The reply is the concatenated message chunks. Thought text stays out of it.

A cancel, a stop, a failure, a `too-large` from the binding, or an exception drops the hold and does not align. The cell still ends on the path it already uses. The five reasons stay. No prompt text is logged.

A death before any forwarded chunk still retries once. Respawn drops the hold. The retry initializes, opens a new session, and sends the whole working set, including when the dead attempt had been a tail. A death after a forwarded chunk, a second death, a parse, a payload, a tool refusal, and an auth failure still do not retry, and they drop the hold. The fifteen-minute reap still destroys the process. The next prompt is a new child, a new session, and the whole working set, on the same desktop boot.

`session/load` is not called. Old session folders under `~/.grok/sessions` for the pool directory stay where Grok wrote them.

The pool plan package 3 keeps the sentence that a reused session would keep the previous prompt.

## 4. Accepted full pastes

These cut a new session and send the working set. They are the goal on those runs.

- The desktop boot id changed, or the boot field is empty.
- The child was just spawned, reaped, or respawned.
- The previous prompt failed, was cancelled, or died.
- The next working set does not start with the held text. That is a re-run of the same card, because that card's own reply stays off its send while the hold already ends with the reply. It is also an edit, an exclude, a state change, a mood change, or a pipe change above the tail. It is a card higher in the column. It is another canvas on the same user, because one child holds one transcript. It is a same-group sibling, because the walker leaves siblings out, so the second member does not start where the first ended. It is a later card whose new context includes a merge this session never saw.
- The next working set equals the hold and the tail would be empty.

A jump downward across several unrun cards is a tail when the shared prefix still matches. The tail can be several role blocks. It is one text block on `session/prompt`, the same role-line shape as the first paste.

## 5. Gotchas the code keeps

Tool refusals stay inside a held session for the life of that id. A new id drops them from the next prompt. This build does not strip them.

The prefix is reconstructed on the server from the text it sent and the reply it stored. It is not read back from the session folder. A pipe token inside a stored reply that resolves to different text on the next Run breaks the prefix, and that Run pastes the working set.

Grok's own system prompt and tool list still ride inside the session on every model call. The saving is the duplicate column, not a smaller agent prompt.

Two pool members of one group stay serial on the child gate. The second member's working set fails the prefix, so it opens its own session. The first member's session is left on disk.

## 6. Proof

## 3. The edit

The button is enabled when the copy source has non-whitespace text. It is disabled when that source is empty or whitespace, so a click leaves the clipboard as it is. Construction uses the same gate as View: `assistantText` trimmed. The first pool chunk that leaves non-whitespace text in the mounted assistant area enables the button. `CellCard` holds the `JButton` so `appendPoolChunk` can enable it.

The copy source on an open card is `assistantArea.getText()`. That area includes in-flight pool kind `c` chunks that `appendPoolChunk` paints before TYPE_DATA stores the reply. The copy source on a collapsed card is `cell.assistantText`. The prompt stays on the card. Thought-strip text stays on the footer. A selection stays a selection. The copy is the whole document.

A folded card during a pool stream copies the last stored reply. `appendPoolChunk` appends those chunks onto the truncated brief and leaves `assistantText` until TYPE_DATA. That catch is accepted. The brief is the eye's line. The copy does not read it.

The View window (`showViewModal`) gains the same icon on the south bar, before Close. The click copies `area.getText()`, the snapshot the dialog opened from `cell.assistantText`. Find highlights and the find field stay. The dialog returns before it is built when the reply is blank, so the View copy button is enabled while the window is up. The dialog title stays as it is.

The control is an icon-only `JButton`. The glyph is two overlapping rounded rectangles, painted in the button foreground and dimmed when the button is disabled. A private method returns an anonymous `Icon`. `fitAetherisButton` keeps the height with the sibling buttons, and `aetheris.short` still survives a font refit. The glyph stays about ten pixels so the seventy-percent height crush leaves it whole. The accessible name is Copy.

The View tip says Copy copies this window's whole reply to the clipboard and the find field stays. Tips go through `buttonTip`.

`groupedMemberWidth` remeasures `headerTop`. The extra button widens the member card and the ten-pixel east gap stays.

## 4. Accepted catch

A collapsed card that is still receiving pool chunks copies the previous stored reply while the brief shows a truncated prefix plus the new chunks. The next TYPE_DATA stores the reply and a rebuild enables Copy on that stored text. An open card copies the painting area the whole time. A whitespace-only reply leaves the button off, the same gate as View.

## 5. Gotchas the code keeps

A busy clipboard is a status line. The slide stays for the failures it already shows.

The copy does not trim a reply that has real text. Leading and trailing blank lines ride along. The enable gate is the trim.

JOIN banners in a merge reply are part of the stored text and part of the View snapshot, so they ride along.

Two buttons do not share one clipboard owner. Each click replaces the clipboard contents. A second click of the same reply writes that reply again.

## 2. What stays

Group, Join, Ungroup, Duplicate group, and Shift-Ctrl-G stay. Clear turns stays the wipe that leaves one empty cell. The copy icon stays. The pool session hold stays on the server. Pipe token text stays as written. Parent pointers stay as stored. Another canvas stays. A replica stays. There is no confirm dialog. There is no new button.

## 3. landing

`VaultStore` and `AetherisNotebookPanel`.

`deleteTurns(canvasId, cellIds, remaining)` performs the writes for many ids in one transaction: trace rows for each id, the cell row on this canvas, turn indexes from 1 on the cells that remain, and the canvas order. `deleteTurn` delegates to that method with a one-element list. Parent pointers and prompt text stay as stored.

On the panel, the removal set is computed from the armed card on the canvas that is showing.

- An empty group id puts that one cell in the set.
- A set group id puts every cell on this canvas with that group id into the set, members and the merge. Column position does not split the set.
- The button is enabled when the set is non-empty and smaller than the canvas. A canvas of one cell stays disabled. A group that is the entire canvas stays disabled.
- The listener still calls `deleteFocusedTurn`. That method recomputes the set and returns when the button would be off.

Before the worker runs, on the Swing thread:

- The ids are buried. `persistAll`, `persistQuiet`, and `saveCells` skip a buried id, because `saveCell` inserts when the update matches zero rows. `applyPendingNow` drops a buried id out of the held answers and does not paint it.
- A RUNNING cell in the set is marked STOPPED and sent the same `TYPE_CANCEL` frame Stop already sends, when that user's connector is open. The spinner stops when that user has no RUNNING cell left.
- Held answers for those ids are removed.
- `chainQueue` is cleared when it contains any removed id.
- `armedGroupSource` is cleared when it names a removed id.

The worker calls `deleteTurns`. On success the Swing thread drops the cells from the canvas and from the order, moves `focusedCellId` to the survivor at the first removed index, or to the last survivor when the set sat at the end, forgets the Grok entries, drops the thinking holders, rebuilds, and refreshes the inspector. The burial stays. The status line says `deleted turn on` plus the canvas name for a normal card, and `deleted group on` plus the canvas name for a group.

On failure the burial for those ids is lifted, the button is refreshed, and the status line says `delete turn failed` or `delete group failed` with the SQL message.

The Delete tip, through `buttonTip`, says a normal card removes that one row, a card in a group removes that whole group and its merge, the survivors are numbered from 1, the button stays off when the canvas would be left empty, and pipe tokens stay as written. The sentence that said the merge sink stays is replaced by this tip.

## 4. Accepted catch

A folded pool stream on a card that is about to leave can still hold chunks in the assistant area. The row is deleted. The chunks are not copied anywhere by this click. A late frame for a buried id is dropped. The server still drops a reply after the cancel by `stillMine`.

A pipe token on a survivor keeps its digits. The index then resolves against the cells that remain.

Deleting the group that sat inside a pool hold does not send a session message.

## 5. Gotchas the code keeps

The burial list lives for the session. A failed delete removes only the ids of that attempt.

A group delete and a one-cell delete share `deleteTurns`. The order of ids in the list does not change the survivor order. The survivor list is the canvas order with those ids removed.

The merge is included because its group id matches. A cell on another canvas is outside the scan.

An unreadable member, a ghost, and every I E P O state ride with the set. State does not split the delete.

The seat is this section.

## 3. landing

`execute` writes the OSC `\u001b]777;select-model;<model>;<think>\u0007`, then `Model Config\r\n`. Model and think come from `getModelName` and `getConsoleThink`. A semicolon, CR, LF, ESC, or BEL inside either string is dropped from the OSC only. The stored strings stay.

**Context.** `CommandContext` gains `getConsoleThink`. `ChatShell` stores `consoleThink`, empty at construct. `applyConsoleModel(String model, String think)` trims the model and, when it is non-empty, calls `setModelName`. The think string is stored as trimmed text, and empty clears it. The method is synchronized on the shell. `getConsoleThink` returns that string, or empty.
**semantic-key:** PK-670
**semantic-key:** PK-670

`shell_model:` replies:

```
shell_model_result:
model: <id>
think: <think>
```

`shell_model_set:` reads a `model:` line and a `think:` line. It calls `applyConsoleModel`, writes `model <id>\r\n` on the VT100, and replies with the same result body. A missing line is empty. The reply does not include the base URL.

**Listener.** `AmwpClientListener` gains `onConsoleModel(String model, String think)`. `SshClientApp.UserFrameListener` forwards it to the notebook on the EDT. `AetherisNotebookPanel` opens the console dialog with those two strings.
**semantic-key:** PK-671

`commitOllamaModel`, the Ollama Thinking listener, and the Ollama effort listener, when `dialogConsole` is set, send `shell_model_set:` and do not call `paintCellTarget` or `persistQuiet`. The think value is empty when the checkbox is off, `true` when it is on and no level is selected, and the level when one is selected. The key listener on the Ollama field does not write a cell while `dialogConsole` is set. Focus lost still commits. `clearModelDialog` clears `dialogConsole`.

`requestConsoleModel` returns when the dialog is already open. When the focused connector is missing or not muxed, the status line is `raw console has no modal`. Otherwise it sends `shell_model:\n` with a nil cell id. `shell_model_result:` in `acceptFrame` opens the console dialog when the dialog is closed. A result that arrives while the dialog is open does not rebuild it.

**Receipt.** The console line after a set is `model` and the id. The status key stays `model`.

## 3. Edits

**Sequences.** `handleCompleteEscape` matches `ESC [ 1; 5 D` and `ESC [ 5 D` for one word backward, and `ESC [ 1; 5 C` and `ESC [ 5 C` for one word forward. A sealed paste and an open paste return before those matches.

**Step.** A word character is a letter or a digit. Forward skips a non-word run, then consumes the next word, and lands on the first character after it. Backward steps onto the previous word and lands on its first character. The line redraws when the cursor changes.

## 3. Edits

**Tries.** A reply that moodPluck can read fills the field and sets the try count to zero. A reply it cannot read counts as one try. The assistant text stays on the glass. The field stays as it was. While the count is under three, the dialog appends one user line and sends the list again. That line asks for one final line that starts with `mood:` and gives the example `mood: spare, dry, lantern-light`. The glass marks that line you. The third reply with no mood line stops. ## 3. Edits

**System line.** `MOOD_SHAPE_SYSTEM` becomes these words. You fill a mood token string. The app stores that string. The app does not store the rest of the reply as the mood. End every reply with one final line. That line is the mood token string. The line starts with mood: and the tokens follow on that same line. Separate the tokens with commas. Each token is one short meaning: a style, a tone, a stance, or a symbol. No stars. No quotes. No code fence on that line. Before that line, say what you determined. When the user asks for more detail, a richer mood, more tokens, or an enriched mood, do both of these. Say the extra detail in the reply, in the words you determined. Put that same detail on the mood line as more tokens. The mood line must contain the new tokens. Do not leave the new detail only in the talk. If the user did not ask for more, a short line is enough. Short example: mood: spare, dry, lantern-light. Enriched example, when the user asked for more: mood: spare, dry, lantern-light, short sentences, no warmth, gravel voice, night watch. When the user asked for more, add tokens until the line holds the detail you named.

**Format ask.** `MOOD_FORMAT_ASK` becomes these words. Return the mood text on one final line. The line starts with mood: and the rest of that line is the mood token string. Separate the tokens with commas. No stars and no quotes on that line. If the user asked for more detail, that line carries more tokens for that detail. Do not leave the detail only in the talk. Short example: mood: spare, dry, lantern-light.

**Tip.** The Mood button tip adds that when you ask for more detail, the mood line is told to carry those extra tokens.
